                        U        S_ Air           Force Summary                    of   Events         and    Information
                     Concerning the                         Unidentified Flying Object Program
            The      Air          Force           feels      a     very definite obligation                              to identify                   and
 analyze
 the
         things that happen                                        In the air  that may have                             in     them       menace
         United             States       and , because                        of     that feeling             of    obligation                  and pur -
  sult      of      that          Interest_    the Air                                   established
  the
                                                                             Force                                 an    activity known                      as
         Unidentified Flying                                Ob ject Program.
            This        program              was      established
                                          In 1947 when unidentified flying
 objects         being reported in various parts
                    were                              of the United     States
 The reports    of; sightings    reached a peak of 1,700 in 1952 and dropped
 to a total    of 429 in 1953_ During the first       nine  months  of 1954 only
 254 sightings     were   reported
            From        a     survey          of'     the
                                                        volume     of sightings    received                                     by the           Air
 Force _       it       has        been determined            that   over   80 percent    are                                   explainable                  as
 being known. objects                                Generally, sighted ob jects fall                                         into        the     cate-
            of:         balloons , aircraft_
 gory                                                                    astronomical                  bodies , atmospheric                       reflec- ~
 tions_           and       birds_            All      reports of unidentified                              flying objects                       result
 from       either           radar           or     visual   sightings .
                        "
            Explanations pertaining                                     to     sightings              reported          from military and
 civilian            radar          facilities                   are     as        follows :
            0
            1
            Temperature inversion       reflections        can                                                                return
                                                                 give a                                                                     on         a
 radar scope   that    1s a8 sharp" a8 that       received     from   an                                                      aircraft.
 Speeds of these returns       reportedly      range from      zero   to                                                      fantastic   rates _
 The "ob jects
          ;       als0   appear to move in all directions_                                                                    Such sightings
 have resulted                    in many fruitless                            intercept efforts .
                     To possibly bear                        out         the
                                                                      theory                     of     temperature    inversion
 reflection                 18    an       incident          which occurred                      in     January 1951 near Oakridge,
Tennessee          Two Air Force     aIrcraft       attempted                                             to intercept    an unidenti

                 An        explanation of                           known           types      of        visual         sightings                 are         as
  follows ;                                                                                                             VZ

                 1_            Present-day jet aircraft,                                       flying             at great speeds             and high
 altitudes,                         are         often       mistaken                 for  unknown   objects                          by the untrained

               2.           Weather                   balloons               account for                 a    substantial               number             of       sight-
 ings _               These               balloons, sent                      to altitudes                    of' 40,000             feet         and

 after dark,                             and     can       be      seen        at very high altitudes.
               3.           In addition   to the ordinary                                               weather balloon,                      huge 90-foot
 balloons ,                  which sometimes    drift   from                                         coast    to         coast,             are       used for
 upper            air         research_   These  balloons                                          also    have          a        highly          reflective
 surface                   and           are     visible                at    extreme          altitudes_                                                           '

hazy           a tmospheric                        conditions                  Since the stars    are   charted                                          and        most

 Meteors                  on       the         other        hand  are   0f rapid  single-direction                                                 movement                and
 are only visible                                 for       a few   seconds _    Meteor activity                                            18     more            common
 at    certain                     times          of       the year than 0 thers, and reports                                                of     UFO's               have
 show            a

                                         arise   which, on the basis    of informatlon         receivec

      received_          It should      be remembered      that    any object     viewed      from   a

          The difficulty                        of        evaluating reports                           of        al1     types is based
 large ly              upon     the      lack        of        basic
                                                 data surrounding                                                       the  sightings. The
drop       In          sightings during 1953    1s largely    due to                                                    the      Increased             accuracy
 and     the           completeness   of reports being    received.                                                      To      be     of     value         a
 report  should                  include
                                 such basic data a8 size,
 speed , altitude
                                                                 shape , composition,
                          direction,     and the maneuver pattern       of  the objects.
Without     such information,          1t 18 almost   impossible     to establish    the
identity     of the object        sighted _    In addition,    a  recent study    has show
3 direct     correlation       between the number of 8ightings reported            and the
publicity given         to    saucers"    by the nation's    press .
          The           Air    Force       took           a
Videon             cameras
                                                                further step                   in early 1953                      by procuring
                                  for      the
                                      purpose     of photographing this phenomena                   These
cameras      were    distributed          to various military        installations _         This type
camera      has two lenses , one of which takes                  an ordinary photograph,              and
 bhe other      has a diffraction             grating    which separates                 into    its
                                                                                light
component parts_               This   aids    in determining the composition               of the     ob-
ject photographed _               A small    number     of photographs  have         been recelved
from    this     camera;       however, only light          spots of no detail         have been
indicated        in  the photos         to date.       As more   photographs       are   taken     by
these observers,            it    18 belleved       that a great deal of the mystery                 will
be lifted       from the program.

 Swedish Pilol Reports 'Saucer'
 By United   PreEs

   STOCKHOLM, Sweden, Dec               18   The   Swedish   Royal Air   Force
 ordered & full investigation today 0f an airliner crew's report 0f seeing
 a saucer-shaped object over Sweden near secret Soviet bases:
   Gen. Bengt Norderskjold,          air force commander-in-chief, called Jn
 complete reports    from     all   Swedish radar stations after joining the
 defense staff in promising          detailed inquiry.
   Capt. Ulf Christiernsson; pilot of the passenger liner; told the defense
 staff he and his crew saw the disc-shaped metallic object shortly after
 noon yesterday over the southern Swedish town of Haessleholm; about
 300 miles from the strategic   Baltic coast:
   "It was entirely    an unorthodox, metallic, symmetrical and ' circular
 object; Capt: Christiernsson said: "I was not at all scared, but curious,
 very curious.
    Capt Christiernsson said the object flew faster than sound about
 5000 feet above the ground:
    Capt: Christiernsson  said the object headed southward over    Jow-
 lying cloud layer which   would obscure it  from ground observers:  He
 said he watched it for about six seconds before it disappeared in the
 direction of East Germany's Baltic shore, near the former Nazi research
 station at Peenemunde, now operated by the Russians:

                FLYING SAUCER|
                OBSERVAtory|
                Not Optical Illusions;|
                 TopExperts Hold
                  OTTAWA      Nov. 12 XCTPS)_
                Establishment   of
                government observatory for fly-
                ing saucers, the first in the world,
                has been announced here_
                  "There is a very high degree of
                probability that flying saucers
                are real objects,   and    60 per
                cent probability   that they  are
                alien vehicles_   Wilbert B. Smith;
                scientist appointed to supervise
                the new saucer sighting station,
                told reporters.
                   He said the federal transport
                department,     in which    he has
                charge of the telecommunications
                broadcast and measurement sec-
                tion, receives constant reports of
                sighting of flying saucers.  The
                total number;     he said, is          classi:
                fied aS restricted information.
                   "The optical illusion             explana-
                tion is lovely, he said, "but in
                every sighting there is always
                some    factor that precludes            this
                explanation _ We have decided to
                try to learn just what they are.
                  Canada's Sighting station               will
                be at Shirley bay on the Ottawa
                river 10 miles west of here. Smith
                said any one locaion in Canada
                is sure to have at least one saucer
                sighting & year. Associated with
                Smith in the project;, "which is
                under the transport department
                and the defense research board_
                will be & theoretical physicist and
                     specialist
                studies
                                  in   gravitational                 Not REOOADED
                   A 24-hour watch will be kept                      145 JAN 4 1964
                for saucers. Specially built equip
                ment is wired to alarm bells. The
                equipment    includes  an   iono-
                spheric reactor; electronic de:
                vices for measurement Of sounds,
                   gamma ray detector;
                meter
                                           gravi-
                                                                 3      Times Herald    22
                       and other paraphernalia.
                  Jet planes may be sent up from                        Wagh . Post
                the air force field near Ottawa
                to investigate any saucers re-
                ported by the station.                                  Wesh . News

                here never have pooh-poohed fly-
                ing saucers, Which have been pub:
                 licly reported in nightmarish
                                                                        Wagh . Star
                 shapes and forms over Canadian
                 cities. Some 0f them have  been                        N.   Y. Timeg
                 described a5 multicolored cigar
                figurations.
                  Frequency of thesaucer sight-                         N. Y.
                ings has been noted here to rise
                with    the increase      in
                                                                                Compegs

                of the planet Mars to the earth

               WHET    FLEW    ACROSS
               ENGLAND    YESTERDAY ?


                    Football
                   Crowds See
             'Flying Saucer' | J0By Sunday Dispatch Reporter

              THOUSANDS         of spectators
                Britain, including people inatmany
                                               foot- parts of
              ball matches, saw what many of them
              believed to be a flying saucer yesterday:
                  In each case the phenomenon ~a      strange
              white flash which darted across the sky at terrific
              speed-~was seen about 4 p.m.
                 These reports of the passage of this_object
              through the skywere received by the Sunday
              Dispatch last night :              match
                                                        NOT RECORDED
                   More than 500 spectators ata Soccer cup             at
              Chard, Somerset,saw         strange white phenomenon  dart  47 JAN 27 1951
              across  the clear sky high above    the grandstandat about
              3.45. Within a split second of passing it seemed to disperse
              on the horizon _

                                 Spectators        Cried 'Oh
                   Spectators     in        cried " Oh
                                       the stand           as the white,
              liquid form sped inland from the direction of the English
              Channel:
                   Spectators at & Rugby match two fields away saw it:
                   One, Mr. Arthur H Jenkins, postmaster 0f Chard, said
                It was like  a diamond flash       its shape   was like   big
              peardrop, with the thin end tapering behind:        Suddenly it
               mened' into nothingness

                                           Prof Giuseppe Belluzzo,, 73-year-old Italian turbine engineer; said to
designs for "flying saucers were prepared for Hitier and Mussolini in 1942.

said      in     an    interview,   "the disks
could carry                      of explosives

Juke Box Birlhed
This Flying Disc,
ArmyExper} Finds
By United Prcss
  SALT LAKE CITY                  July 12        The
"Flying Disc" that fell with a thud
in the yard       of
              a Twin Falls, Idaho,
housewife was found today to have
been manufactured                by four        teen-
age boys      from        the parts of          run-
down juke box:
  Discovery of the disc                   was     an-
nounced yesterday by an agent of
the Federal Bureau of Investigation
who      turned    it     ovcr   to   Army      offi:
cers    at Twin         Falls.   They in turn
put it aboard a plane and rushed it
to       Douglas near here for ex
amination by experts:
   The      examination           showed         the
fancy gadget complete with plexi-
glass      dome,        three     radio      tubes,
chromium-plated edges                     and    nu-
merous wires          a hoax and, ac
                         was
tording to the Army had "no oth
 r function   than to be ornamental
    a. limited manner and    to cause
considerable expense to Federal
                    it:
agencies investigating
